Hi. Thank you so much for taking the time to see me. Um, I hope it's okay that I'm here. It's probably nothing major, honestly.

Um, well... I wouldn't say it's *interfering*, exactly. I'm still doing my job and everything! I really try to stay on top of things.

Um, checking... well, I don't really count or anything. I just want to look professional, you know? 

I guess... before I have to send out event pictures or, like, right before I hop on a video call with a vendor, I'll just do a quick check in my phone camera. Just to make sure nothing looks crazy. I guess sometimes it does take a little longer than I mean for it to, and that's when I might fall a tiny bit behind on some of the planning stuff.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, a certain number of times? No, definitely not like a set number or anything rigid like that. I'm not, like, counting down or doing a routine. 

I just... I guess I just keep checking until I feel like it's safe? Like I mentioned before, if I have to send out those event pictures or jump on a vendor meeting, I'll just keep pulling up my phone camera or retaking the photos until I can find an angle where my face doesn't look... off. I'll just keep tilting my head and adjusting until it feels okay enough to proceed. 

And sometimes, yeah, I'll keep asking my coworker if it looks weird until they reassure me. It's really just until I feel presentable enough to do my job. It's just that sometimes getting to that point takes... well, it can eat up enough time that I fall behind on the planning stuff. 

But I'm not doing it a specific number of times! Just until I look professional. That's a pretty normal reason to check, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job representing the company.

Um... judge me? I don't think they'd, like, judge me harshly or think I'm a terrible person or anything. People are usually pretty nice.

I guess I do check it sometimes. Not, like, all the time or anything crazy! Just... before I send out event pictures to clients, or if I have to jump on a video call with a vendor. I'll just, you know, quickly check my phone camera to make sure it doesn't look too obvious. \n\nSometimes I might retake a photo a few times, or try to tilt my head a certain way so it doesn't catch the light wrong. Like, I'm sure I've wished I was a little taller or something normal like that, but... no, I don't obsess over anything else. I try to stay pretty positive about myself overall. I don't think I'd call it a lot of distress. I definitely don't want to sound dramatic or, like, ungrateful for what I have. \n\nIt's really just... frustrating, I guess? Like, I'm still getting my events put together, and I'm still showing up to work every day with a smile on my face. I'm definitely not sitting around crying about it or anything crazy like that. I just... I get a little flustered when I see a bad picture or catch my reflection wrong. I wouldn't say it's *interfering*, exactly. I'm still doing my job and everything! I spent a little too much time trying to make sure I looked okay in some of the event materials we were putting together, and the time just kind of got away from me. \n\nAnd, um, I guess getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings does take me a little longer than it probably should, just because I want to make sure the lighting is right and everything. But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I just want to look professional, you know? \n\nI guess... before I have to send out event pictures or, like, right before I hop on a video call with a vendor, I'll just do a quick check in my phone camera. I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. I guess sometimes it does take a little longer than I mean for it to, and that's when I might fall a tiny bit behind on some of the planning stuff. \n\nBut I always catch up! Um... wow, that's a hard question. If nobody could see it, not even me? 

I guess... I don't really know? I've never really thought about it like that, because, well, I can always see it, right? Even if I'm not looking in a mirror, I just sort of... know it's there.

Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right. It's kind of a confusing thing to imagine, but I want to make sure I'm giving you a good answer. Is that what you were asking?

I know it's important to have a good self-image, especially in my line of work where you have to be upbeat for clients. \n\nSo, yeah, it's really just that one little facial thing that's been bugging me lately. Is that... is it normal for it to just be one specific area like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your face been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... distress? I don't think I'd call it a lot of distress. I definitely don't want to sound dramatic or, like, ungrateful for what I have. \n\nIt's really just... frustrating, I guess? Like, I'm still getting my events put together, and I'm still showing up to work every day with a smile on my face. I'm definitely not sitting around crying about it or anything crazy like that. I just... I get a little flustered when I see a bad picture or catch my reflection wrong. But I'm handling it okay, I think. \n\nI really hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. Does that sound like a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your face been interfering with your work or daily activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's *interfering*, exactly. I'm still doing my job and everything! I really try to stay on top of things. \n\nI mean, I guess... there was this one time recently where I missed a small part of a planning deadline. But it wasn't a huge deal! It's just... I spent a little too much time trying to make sure I looked okay in some of the event materials we were putting together, and the time just kind of got away from me. \n\nAnd, um, I guess getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings does take me a little longer than it probably should, just because I want to make sure the lighting is right and everything. But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm bad at my job.\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you check your face in a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um, checking... well, I don't really count or anything. I just want to look professional, you know? \n\nI guess... before I have to send out event pictures or, like, right before I hop on a video call with a vendor, I'll just do a quick check in my phone camera. Just to make sure nothing looks crazy. Sometimes I'll take a few photos, or, um, retake them a couple of times just to make sure the angle isn't making my face look weird. I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. It's really just to make sure I'm presenting myself well for the clients! But... I guess sometimes it does take a little longer than I mean for it to, and that's when I might fall a tiny bit behind on some of the planning stuff. \n\nBut I always catch up! It's not like I'm staring at mirrors all day. Just... normal touch-ups, right? Everyone checks their phone camera before a meeting, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your face a certain number of times before you feel okay?\n\nPatient: Um, a certain number of times? No, definitely not like a set number or anything rigid like that. I'm not, like, counting down or doing a routine. \n\nI just... I guess I just keep checking until I feel like it's safe? Like I mentioned before, if I have to send out those event pictures or jump on a vendor meeting, I'll just keep pulling up my phone camera or retaking the photos until I can find an angle where my face doesn't look... off. I'll just keep tilting my head and adjusting until it feels okay enough to proceed. \n\nAnd sometimes, yeah, I'll keep asking my coworker if it looks weird until they reassure me. It's really just until I feel presentable enough to do my job. It's just that sometimes getting to that point takes... well, it can eat up enough time that I fall behind on the planning stuff. \n\nBut I'm not doing it a specific number of times! Just until I look professional. That's a pretty normal reason to check, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job representing the company.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look okay to others even if you didn't check it?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, probably? Like, logically, I know my coworkers aren't staring at me or anything. They're just doing their jobs. \n\nBut... I guess I just worry that if I don't check, I might have caught some terrible lighting or I'm sitting at a really bad angle, and then someone *will* notice. And I really don't want to make people uncomfortable or look unprofessional. So I just... I check to be safe. It's just a little peace of mind, you know? \n\nI'm sure it would probably be fine, mostly. I just... I feel better when I know for sure. Does that make sense? I just want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you or think less of you if they saw your face without you checking and preparing it first?\n\nPatient: Um... judge me? I don't think they'd, like, judge me harshly or think I'm a terrible person or anything. People are usually pretty nice. \n\nI just... I guess I worry they might think I'm not put together? Like, if I'm on a video call and the angle is bad, they might think I don't take my job seriously, or that I'm messy. I don't think they'd be mean about it, but... I just want to make a good impression, you know? \n\nI really try hard to be a good event planner, and looking the part is just... it's important, right? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm sloppy. I want to feel okay about myself, even if nobody else is around. If I walk past the mirror and I look, you know, uneven or just... wrong, it still bothers me. I still feel that urge to just fix it really quick so I can focus on other things. \n\nIt's not like I'm obsessed with it or anything, it's just... a quick check to make sure things are okay. Um... gosh, if I was absolutely certain? I guess... I don't really know how to answer that one. 

It's just really hard to imagine, because... well, I do see things that look off, so I've never really had a moment where I felt completely certain that everything was perfectly fine. I suppose if I genuinely believed there was nothing wrong at all, maybe I wouldn't need to check? But my brain just kind of always tells me I need to make sure. 

I'm sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very good answer for this one. I just haven't ever really experienced feeling that way, you know? I hope that's okay to say. I'm really trying to be helpful, but that hypothetical is just a little tough for me to wrap my head around. Is it... is it normal to not really know how to answer that?

Like, I'm sure I've wished I was a little taller or something normal like that, but... no, I don't obsess over anything else. I try to stay pretty positive about myself overall. I know it's important to have a good self-image, especially in my line of work where you have to be upbeat for clients. \n\nSo, yeah, it's really just that one little facial thing that's been bugging me lately. Is that... is it normal for it to just be one specific area like that?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your face been causing you a lot of distress?\n\nPatient: Um... distress? I don't think I'd call it a lot of distress. I definitely don't want to sound dramatic or, like, ungrateful for what I have. \n\nIt's really just... frustrating, I guess? Like, I'm still getting my events put together, and I'm still showing up to work every day with a smile on my face. I'm definitely not sitting around crying about it or anything crazy like that. I just... I get a little flustered when I see a bad picture or catch my reflection wrong. But I'm handling it okay, I think. \n\nI really hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. Does that sound like a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your face been interfering with your work or daily activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's *interfering*, exactly. I'm still doing my job and everything! I really try to stay on top of things. \n\nI mean, I guess... there was this one time recently where I missed a small part of a planning deadline. But it wasn't a huge deal! It's just... I spent a little too much time trying to make sure I looked okay in some of the event materials we were putting together, and the time just kind of got away from me. \n\nAnd, um, I guess getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings does take me a little longer than it probably should, just because I want to make sure the lighting is right and everything. But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. Like I mentioned before, if I have to send out those event pictures or jump on a vendor meeting, I'll just keep pulling up my phone camera or retaking the photos until I can find an angle where my face doesn't look... off. I'll just keep tilting my head and adjusting until it feels okay enough to proceed. \n\nAnd sometimes, yeah, I'll keep asking my coworker if it looks weird until they reassure me. It's really just until I feel presentable enough to do my job. I just want to make a good impression, you know? \n\nI really try hard to be a good event planner, and looking the part is just... it's important, right? But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm bad at my job.\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you check your face in a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um, checking... well, I don't really count or anything. I just want to look professional, you know? \n\nI guess... before I have to send out event pictures or, like, right before I hop on a video call with a vendor, I'll just do a quick check in my phone camera. Just to make sure nothing looks crazy. Sometimes I'll take a few photos, or, um, retake them a couple of times just to make sure the angle isn't making my face look weird. I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. It's really just to make sure I'm presenting myself well for the clients! But... I guess sometimes it does take a little longer than I mean for it to, and that's when I might fall a tiny bit behind on some of the planning stuff. \n\nBut I always catch up! It's not like I'm staring at mirrors all day. Just... normal touch-ups, right? Everyone checks their phone camera before a meeting, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your face a certain number of times before you feel okay?\n\nPatient: Um, a certain number of times? No, definitely not like a set number or anything rigid like that. I'm not, like, counting down or doing a routine. \n\nI just... I guess I just keep checking until I feel like it's safe? Like I mentioned before, if I have to send out those event pictures or jump on a vendor meeting, I'll just keep pulling up my phone camera or retaking the photos until I can find an angle where my face doesn't look... off. I'll just keep tilting my head and adjusting until it feels okay enough to proceed. \n\nAnd sometimes, yeah, I'll keep asking my coworker if it looks weird until they reassure me. It's really just until I feel presentable enough to do my job. It's just that sometimes getting to that point takes... well, it can eat up enough time that I fall behind on the planning stuff. \n\nBut I'm not doing it a specific number of times! Just until I look professional. That's a pretty normal reason to check, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job representing the company.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look okay to others even if you didn't check it?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, probably? Like, logically, I know my coworkers aren't staring at me or anything. They're just doing their jobs. \n\nBut... I guess I just worry that if I don't check, I might have caught some terrible lighting or I'm sitting at a really bad angle, and then someone *will* notice. And I really don't want to make people uncomfortable or look unprofessional. So I just... I check to be safe. It's just a little peace of mind, you know? \n\nI'm sure it would probably be fine, mostly. I just... I feel better when I know for sure. Does that make sense? I just want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you or think less of you if they saw your face without you checking and preparing it first?\n\nPatient: Um... judge me? I don't think they'd, like, judge me harshly or think I'm a terrible person or anything. People are usually pretty nice. \n\nI just... I guess I worry they might think I'm not put together? Like, if I'm on a video call and the angle is bad, they might think I don't take my job seriously, or that I'm messy. I don't think they'd be mean about it, but... I just want to make a good impression, you know? \n\nI really try hard to be a good event planner, and looking the part is just... it's important, right? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm sloppy. That's a normal thing to worry about in a professional setting, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your face is mostly about how you feel about yourself, or about how others might see you?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe mostly about how I feel about myself? \n\nI mean, I definitely want to look professional for other people, like I said. But even when I'm just by myself, working from home on a Saturday or something, I still feel... I still feel like I need to check. Just to make sure I don't look completely awful. \n\nIf I'm being honest, it just makes me feel really down if I catch a bad reflection or a bad picture. It just ruins my mood. So I try to fix it so I don't have to feel that way. \n\nI'm sorry, I don't know if I'm explaining that very well. Does that sound like it's more about me or other people? I just want to give you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face if you were completely alone and didn't have to interact with anyone?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. Like I was saying, even on a weekend when I'm just home alone and not seeing anyone, I still... I still catch myself doing it. \n\nI guess I just... I want to feel okay about myself, even if nobody else is around. If I walk past the mirror and I look, you know, uneven or just... wrong, it still bothers me. I still feel that urge to just fix it really quick so I can focus on other things. \n\nIt's not like I'm obsessed with it or anything, it's just... a quick check to make sure things are okay. That's pretty normal, right? Like, wanting to feel comfortable in your own skin even when you're alone? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to fix your face even if nobody, including yourself, could see it?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a hard question. If nobody could see it, not even me? \n\nI guess... I don't really know? I've never really thought about it like that, because, well, I can always see it, right? Even if I'm not looking in a mirror, I just sort of... know it's there. \n\nBut if I couldn't see it at all... maybe I wouldn't need to fix it? Because if I can't see that it looks bad, then maybe it wouldn't bother me as much. I think most of the time it's just seeing it that makes me feel like I have to do something about it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right. It's kind of a confusing thing to imagine, but I want to make sure I'm giving you a good answer. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or compelled to check your face if you were certain that nobody would notice or care about the uneven part you're concerned about?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I probably still would? Because even if I knew for sure they wouldn't care, I still feel like I have to present myself a certain way, you know? \n\nLike, I'm an event planner, so I'm always dealing with vendors and sending out pictures of venues and stuff. And before I join a video call or send out any photos where I might be in the frame, I just... I kind of have to check my phone camera a lot. I'll tilt my head to find the right angle, or sometimes I'll take a few different pictures until I get one that looks okay. Or I might just quietly ask a coworker if my face looks weird that day. \n\nIt's just to be professional! But... I guess sometimes I do spend a little too much time doing it, and then I have to rush to catch up on my actual planning work. It's not a huge deal, but it does eat up some time. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face if you were absolutely certain that there was nothing actually wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, if I was absolutely certain? I guess... I don't really know how to answer that one. \n\nIt's just really hard to imagine, because... well, I do see things that look off, so I've never really had a moment where I felt completely certain that everything was perfectly fine. I suppose if I genuinely believed there was nothing wrong at all, maybe I wouldn't need to check? But my brain just kind of always tells me I need to make sure. \n\nI'm sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very good answer for this one. I just haven't ever really experienced feeling that way, you know? I hope that's okay to say. I'm really trying to be helpful, but that hypothetical is just a little tough for me to wrap my head around. Is it... is it normal to not really know how to answer that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your need to check your face is more about fixing the part you think is uneven or about avoiding what others might think of you if they see it?\n\nPatient: Um... that's a really good question. I think... well, maybe it's a little bit of both? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. But I feel like I'd still be really anxious about it. But I'm handling it okay, I think. \n\nI really hope I'm not making a bigger deal out of this than I should be. Does that sound like a normal way to feel about it?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your face been interfering with your work or daily activities in any way?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say it's *interfering*, exactly. I'm still doing my job and everything! I really try to stay on top of things. \n\nI mean, I guess... there was this one time recently where I missed a small part of a planning deadline. But it wasn't a huge deal! It's just... I spent a little too much time trying to make sure I looked okay in some of the event materials we were putting together, and the time just kind of got away from me. \n\nAnd, um, I guess getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings does take me a little longer than it probably should, just because I want to make sure the lighting is right and everything. But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm bad at my job.\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you check your face in a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um, checking... well, I don't really count or anything. I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. It's really just to make sure I'm presenting myself well for the clients! But... I spent a little too much time trying to make sure I looked okay in some of the event materials we were putting together, and the time just kind of got away from me. \n\nAnd, um, I guess getting ready for vendor calls and prepping for meetings does take me a little longer than it probably should, just because I want to make sure the lighting is right and everything. But I'm still getting it all done! I really try hard not to let it affect my work too much. I want to be a good employee, you know? \n\nDoes that... does that make sense? I hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm bad at my job.\n\nDoctor: How often would you say you check your face in a typical day?\n\nPatient: Um, checking... well, I don't really count or anything. I just want to look professional, you know? \n\nI guess... before I have to send out event pictures or, like, right before I hop on a video call with a vendor, I'll just do a quick check in my phone camera. Just to make sure nothing looks crazy. Sometimes I'll take a few photos, or, um, retake them a couple of times just to make sure the angle isn't making my face look weird. I'll kind of tilt my head a certain way until it looks okay. \n\nAnd, um, sometimes I might ask one of my coworkers if I look alright, just to get a second opinion. It's really just to make sure I'm presenting myself well for the clients! But... I guess sometimes it does take a little longer than I mean for it to, and that's when I might fall a tiny bit behind on some of the planning stuff. \n\nBut I always catch up! It's not like I'm staring at mirrors all day. Just... normal touch-ups, right? Everyone checks their phone camera before a meeting, don't they?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to check your face a certain number of times before you feel okay?\n\nPatient: Um, a certain number of times? No, definitely not like a set number or anything rigid like that. I'm not, like, counting down or doing a routine. \n\nI just... I guess I just keep checking until I feel like it's safe? Like I mentioned before, if I have to send out those event pictures or jump on a vendor meeting, I'll just keep pulling up my phone camera or retaking the photos until I can find an angle where my face doesn't look... off. I'll just keep tilting my head and adjusting until it feels okay enough to proceed. \n\nAnd sometimes, yeah, I'll keep asking my coworker if it looks weird until they reassure me. It's really just until I feel presentable enough to do my job. It's just that sometimes getting to that point takes... well, it can eat up enough time that I fall behind on the planning stuff. \n\nBut I'm not doing it a specific number of times! Just until I look professional. That's a pretty normal reason to check, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing a good job representing the company.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your face would look okay to others even if you didn't check it?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, probably? Like, logically, I know my coworkers aren't staring at me or anything. They're just doing their jobs. \n\nBut... I guess I just worry that if I don't check, I might have caught some terrible lighting or I'm sitting at a really bad angle, and then someone *will* notice. And I really don't want to make people uncomfortable or look unprofessional. So I just... I check to be safe. It's just a little peace of mind, you know? \n\nI'm sure it would probably be fine, mostly. I just... I feel better when I know for sure. Does that make sense? I just want to do things right.\n\nDoctor: Do you think people would judge you or think less of you if they saw your face without you checking and preparing it first?\n\nPatient: Um... judge me? I don't think they'd, like, judge me harshly or think I'm a terrible person or anything. People are usually pretty nice. \n\nI just... I guess I worry they might think I'm not put together? Like, if I'm on a video call and the angle is bad, they might think I don't take my job seriously, or that I'm messy. I don't think they'd be mean about it, but... I just want to make a good impression, you know? \n\nI really try hard to be a good event planner, and looking the part is just... it's important, right? I just wouldn't want anyone to think I'm sloppy. That's a normal thing to worry about in a professional setting, don't you think?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your concern about your face is mostly about how you feel about yourself, or about how others might see you?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess it's a little bit of both? But maybe mostly about how I feel about myself? \n\nI mean, I definitely want to look professional for other people, like I said. But even when I'm just by myself, working from home on a Saturday or something, I still feel... I still feel like I need to check. Just to make sure I don't look completely awful. \n\nIf I'm being honest, it just makes me feel really down if I catch a bad reflection or a bad picture. It just ruins my mood. So I try to fix it so I don't have to feel that way. \n\nI'm sorry, I don't know if I'm explaining that very well. Does that sound like it's more about me or other people? I just want to give you the right answer.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face if you were completely alone and didn't have to interact with anyone?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I think so. Like I was saying, even on a weekend when I'm just home alone and not seeing anyone, I still... I still catch myself doing it. \n\nI guess I just... I want to feel okay about myself, even if nobody else is around. If I walk past the mirror and I look, you know, uneven or just... wrong, it still bothers me. I still feel that urge to just fix it really quick so I can focus on other things. \n\nIt's not like I'm obsessed with it or anything, it's just... a quick check to make sure things are okay. That's pretty normal, right? Like, wanting to feel comfortable in your own skin even when you're alone? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. Is that helpful?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still want to fix your face even if nobody, including yourself, could see it?\n\nPatient: Um... wow, that's a hard question. If nobody could see it, not even me? \n\nI guess... I don't really know? I've never really thought about it like that, because, well, I can always see it, right? Even if I'm not looking in a mirror, I just sort of... know it's there. \n\nBut if I couldn't see it at all... maybe I wouldn't need to fix it? Because if I can't see that it looks bad, then maybe it wouldn't bother me as much. I think most of the time it's just seeing it that makes me feel like I have to do something about it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm answering that right. It's kind of a confusing thing to imagine, but I want to make sure I'm giving you a good answer. Is that what you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel anxious or compelled to check your face if you were certain that nobody would notice or care about the uneven part you're concerned about?\n\nPatient: Um... I think I probably still would? Because even if I knew for sure they wouldn't care, I still feel like I have to present myself a certain way, you know? \n\nLike, I'm an event planner, so I'm always dealing with vendors and sending out pictures of venues and stuff. And before I join a video call or send out any photos where I might be in the frame, I just... I kind of have to check my phone camera a lot. I'll tilt my head to find the right angle, or sometimes I'll take a few different pictures until I get one that looks okay. Or I might just quietly ask a coworker if my face looks weird that day. \n\nIt's just to be professional! But... I guess sometimes I do spend a little too much time doing it, and then I have to rush to catch up on my actual planning work. It's not a huge deal, but it does eat up some time. \n\nI just want to make sure I'm putting my best foot forward. Is that... is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is, I just want to be helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face if you were absolutely certain that there was nothing actually wrong with your appearance?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, if I was absolutely certain? I guess... I don't really know how to answer that one. \n\nIt's just really hard to imagine, because... well, I do see things that look off, so I've never really had a moment where I felt completely certain that everything was perfectly fine. I suppose if I genuinely believed there was nothing wrong at all, maybe I wouldn't need to check? But my brain just kind of always tells me I need to make sure. \n\nI'm sorry, I feel like I'm not giving you a very good answer for this one. I just haven't ever really experienced feeling that way, you know? I hope that's okay to say. I'm really trying to be helpful, but that hypothetical is just a little tough for me to wrap my head around. Is it... is it normal to not really know how to answer that?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your need to check your face is more about fixing the part you think is uneven or about avoiding what others might think of you if they see it?\n\nPatient: Um... that's a really good question. I think... well, maybe it's a little bit of both? But honestly, I think it's mostly about just wanting to fix it for myself. \n\nBecause even when I'm just home alone, I still kind of feel like I need to check it and see if it looks okay, or if there's something I can do to make it look a little more even. It just makes me feel better when things look right, you know? \n\nI mean, I definitely don't want people to think I look messy or unprofessional when I'm working, but... I don't think I'm constantly terrified of what they're thinking. It's more just this little nagging feeling I have that I need to fix it. \n\nDoes that make sense? I hope I'm explaining it right. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the information you need. Am I answering your questions okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your need to check your face would decrease if you couldn't see yourself, but others could still see you?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow, if I couldn't see myself at all? I guess... I've never really thought about it like that. \n\nI think... maybe it would decrease? Because if there's no mirror or camera, I literally can't check, right? But I feel like I'd still be really anxious about it. Like, I'd probably still be wondering if my face looked uneven, especially if I knew other people were looking at me during a meeting or something. \n\nBut without being able to actually look and try to fix it, I guess the physical checking part would have to stop. I don't know if that means the feeling would go away, though. \n\nI'm sorry, I feel like my answers to these hypothetical ones are a little all over the place. I just want to make sure I'm giving you what you need to figure this out. Is that... is that an okay answer? I hope I'm not being too confusing.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your need to check your face would be less if you were in a situation where you knew you would not be evaluated or judged by others?\n\nPatient: Um... gosh, I'm honestly not too sure about that one either. Because, well, like I mentioned a little bit ago, I still do it when I'm just home by myself, where I know absolutely no one is evaluating me or looking at me. \n\nSo... maybe it wouldn't be less? Or maybe it would be a tiny bit less just because there's no pressure from work or anything? I just haven't really noticed a huge difference based on who's around, to be perfectly honest. \n\nI'm so sorry, I feel like I'm doing a terrible job with these hypothetical questions today! I really want to give you a solid answer, but I just don't really know for sure. Is it... is it okay if I just say I'm not sure? I hope I'm still being somewhat helpful.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you would still feel the need to check your face if you were certain that you looked perfect to others?\n\nPatient: Um... oh, wow, perfect to others? That's... that's a really interesting way to think about it. \n\nI guess... well, if I knew for a fact that everyone else thought I looked completely fine, maybe the urge wouldn't be quite as strong? But... I don't know, I feel like I'd still probably check. Because, like I said, it's really about me feeling like it's uneven, you know? Even if someone told me it looked perfect, if I look in the mirror and it still seems off to me, I think I'd still want to try and fix it. \n\nI really hope I'm not sounding too vain or obsessed with myself by saying that! ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... if I could just completely accept it? Wow, that sounds... really nice, actually.
